परिभापाएं : 


( 1 ) विजातीय पदार्थ : इसके अन्तर्गत धूल , पत्थर, मिट्टी के ढेले, भूसी, तिनक और अन्य कोई गंदगी पाएगी ।

___ 4. निरीक्षण की प्रकिया :- - ( 1 ) ऐसा कोई निर्यातकर्ता जो बासमता

( 2 ) परिषद द्वारा और ए में प्रमभरण समिट छारा जिसे इस प्रयोग के

के लिए मानिन बासमती चावल का परेषण परिषद् दाग पा

3. निरीक्षक का आधार - याममती घायल का निरीक्षण यह देने 
की दृष्टि से किया जाएगा कि वह अधिनियम की धार। 6 के प्रधान केन्द्रीय 
सरकार द्वारा मान्यताप्राप्त मामक विनिर्दोशी के प्रनम्हप है, जो 
या सो ( क ) यह सनिपिनस करके किया जाएगा कि उत्पाद 
का प्रसंस्करण परिषद द्वारा यथा निर्धारित विरमक ग्रंश प्रकिना 
क्वालिटी मियंत्रण का प्रयोग करते हुए किया गया है ; 


( ख ) ऐसी दशा में , जहां प्रसंस्करणकर्ता नियतिका नही है 
परेषण का भौतिक रूप से सत्यापन किया जाएगा 
पौर ऐसा सत्यापन और/ या निरीक्षण यदि कोई प्रावश्यक 
हो , अभिकरण द्वारा यह सुनिश्चित करने के लिए 
किया जाएगा कि उपरोक्त शो का अनुपालन किया गया 
है । 


( ख ) परिषद द्वारा विहित रीति से किए गए निरीक्षण तथा परीक्षण 

के प्राधार पर किया गया है । 


[ भाग II .-- Us 3 ( ii )] 


भारत का राजपत्र : फरवरी 7, 1990/माघ 28, 1911 


7. निरीक्षण फीस: - -निरीक्षण के प्रयोजन के लिए निर्धारित दरों 
पर फीस भाभिषारण को क्रमश : परेषणानुसार निरीक्षण और मन्त प्रक्रिया 
क्वालिटी नियंत्रण के लिए निरीक्षण फीस के रुप में दी जाएगी 


____ 5. ( क ) मभिकरण, फिर भी , निर्यात के लिए प्राथयित किनहीं 

परेषणों को स्थल पर जांच करेगा और यूनिट द्वारा अपनाए गए 
प्रसंस्करण अंत : प्रक्रिया ग्वालिटी नियंत्रण ड्रिसों को पर्याप्तता 
को जांच करने के लिए नियमित अन्तरालों पर प्रसंस्करण 
एफकों में आए गा । यदि यह पाया जाता है कि प्रसंस्करण 
यूनिट ने प्रसंस्करण के किसी भी स्तर पर प्रपेक्षित 
पालिटी नियंत्रण उपायों को नहीं अपनाया है या परिषद 
अभिकरण की सिफारिशों का अनुपालन नहीं किया है 
तो यह घोषित किया जाएगा कि यूनिट के पास संस्करण 
गत पर्याप्त क्वालिटी नियंत्रण ट्रिलें नही है ऐसे 
मामलों में यदि यूनिट ऐसा चाहता है तो यह अन्तः 
प्रक्रिया क्वालिटी नियंत्रण शिलों की पर्याप्त व्यवस्था को बनाएं 

रखने के लिए नए सिरे से प्रावेदन करेगा । 
( ख ) ऐसे मामलों में , जहां निर्यातकर्ता ने उप नियम ( 2 ) के 

अधीन यह घोषित नहीं किया है कि परिषद् द्वारा 
मधिकथित पर्याप्त स्वालिटी नियंत्रण का प्रयोग किया 
गया है, अभिकरण अपना यह समाधान कर ले ने पर 
कि बासमती चावल का परेषण परिषद् द्वारा अधिकथित 
के अनुसार किए गए, निरीक्षण तथा परीक्षणों के प्राधार 
पर इस प्रयोजन के लिए मान्यता प्राप्त मानक 
विनिर्देशों के अनुरूप है, तो वह यथा स्थिति 2 या 10 
दिनों के भीतर, बासमती चावल के परेषण को निर्यात योग्य 
घोषित करते हुए प्रमाणपत्र जारी कर देगा । 


8 . अपील : --- 
( क ) ऐसा कोई निर्यातकर्ता जो भिकरण द्वारा निरीक्षण प्रमाण 

पत्र जारी करने से इंकार करने से व्यथित है 
ऐसे इंकार की सूचना प्राप्त होने के दस दिनों के 
भीतर इस प्रयोजन के लिए गठित विशेषज्ञों के 

पैनल को अपील कर सकेगा । 
( प ) पैनल की गणपूर्ति तीन से होगी, 
( ग ) अपील प्राप्त होने के पन्द्रह दिनों के भीतर निपटा 

वी जाएगी । 
( ध ) ऐसे अपीलों में पेनल का विनिश्चय अंतिम माना जाएगा । 

[ फाइल सं . 6 ( 13/ 87-ई . प्राई एण्ड ईपी )]

MINISTRY OF COMMERCE 

ORDER 
New Delhi, the 17th February , 1990 


परन्तु जहाँ अभिकरण का ऐसा समाधान नहीं होता है 
वहां वह निर्यातकर्ता को प्रमाणपन्न देने से इंकार कर देगा और 
यह घोषणा करेगा कि बागमती चावल परेषण निर्याप्त योग्य 
नही है , और यथा स्थिति 2 या 10 दिन के भीतर इंकार किए 
जाने के कारणों सहित निर्यातकर्ता को इंकार की सूचना 
देगा । 


S . O . 408. - Whereas in exercise of the powers conferred 
hy Section 6 of the Export ( Quality Control & Inspection ) 
Act , 1963 ( 22 of 1963 ), the Central Government is of the 
opinion that it is necessary and expedient so to do for the 
development of the Export Trade of India that Basmati Rice 
should be subject to quality control and inspection prior 
to export ; 


And whereas the Central Government has formulated the 
proposals specificd below for the sai purpose and has for 
warded the game to the Export Inspection Council as required 
by sub -rule ( 2 ) of rule 11 of the Export (Quality Control and 
Inspection) Rules, 1964. 


____ Now, therefore , in pursuance of the said sub - rule the Cen 
tral Government , in supersession of the notification of the 
Government of India in the Ministry of Commerce S . O . No . 
1025 And 1026 dated 19th April, 1980 as amended by S. O . 
824 ( E ) dated 30th September, 1980 and S. O . 3916 dated 
15th October , 1983 relating to Basmati Rice , hereby publishes 
the said proposals for the information of the public likely to 
be affected thereby . 


6. प्रमाणीकरण के अभिकरण को भण्डारण के किसी भी स्थान 
पर वास्तविक लदान से पहले परेषण की क्वालिटी का पुन : 
गूल्यांकन परिवहन में पत्तन पर करने का भी अधिकार होगा । 

7. यदि, परषण को इनमें से पिसी भी स्तर पर मामक विनिर्देशों 
के अनुरूप नहीं पाया जाता है तो मूल रूप से दिया गया निरीक्षण 
प्रमाणपत्न वापिस ले लिया जाएगा । 

5 . पंकिंग करना और चिम्हॉफन : - - परेपण को नियमानुसार तैयार 
धारने के पश्चात निर्यातकर्तानिर्यात के लिए बासमती चावल को पैक करना 
चाहता है तो स्टैई नए गनी थैलों / अंदर की मोर पोलियोन 
सहित कन्धस येलों / अधिक सतह वाले पोलीलेमिनेटिड कार्डबाई 
हिट्यो मे या क्रेता की विशिष्ट अपभागों के अनुरूप पंक करेगा । 

थैलों / पंजों पर निम्नलिग्निन गूचना स्टैमिल अंकित मदिस 
रहेगी : 

1. नितिकर्स का नाम और पता ; 
2. विविध किस्में : 
3. श्रेणो : 
4. लौट संख्या : 
5. कुा भार तथा शुद्ध भार : 
6 . भारत का उतपादन : 
7 गिपिग चिन्दन । 

6. निरीक्षण का स्थान :- - इन नियमों के प्रयोजन के लिए 
निरीक्षण निर्यातकर्ता के उस परिमर पर किया जाएगा जहा 
माल निरीक्षक के लिए प्रस्तुत किया जाता है परन्तु यह तब मम कि 
यहां निरीक्षण के लिए पर्याप्त मुविधाएं विद्यमान हों । 


2 . Notice is hereby given that any person desiring to for 
ward any objections or suggestions with respect to the said 
proposals may forward the same within fortyfive days from 
the date of publication of this order in the Official Gazette to 
the Export Inspection Council, Pragati Tower, 11th Floor , 
26, Rajendra Place , New Delhi - 110008. 


PROPOSALS 


( 1) To notify that Basinati Rice shall be subject to quality 
control and inspection prior to export, 

( 2 ) To specify the type of quality control and inspection 
in accordance with the draft of the proposed Export of Bus 
mati Rice (Inspection ) Rules, 1990 us set out in the Annexure 
appended to This Order , as the type of quality control and 
inspection which sh : ]l le applied to such Basmati Rice prior 
10 their export ; 
( 3 ) To recognisc 
(a ) The pational stapdards of importing countries and 

international standards ;

ANNEXURE 


formg to the standard specifications recognised by the Cen 
tral Govornment under Section 6 of the Act, either 


Draft rules proposed to be made under Section 17 of tho 
Export (Quality Control & Inspection ) Act, 1963 (22 of 1963 ) 
in supersession of the notification No . S . O . 1026 dated 19th 
April , 1980 , cxcept in respect of things done or omitted to 
be done before such supersession . 


( a ) By casuring that the product has been processed by 

cxercising necessary in - process quality controls as 
prescribed by the Council ; 


(b ) On tlic basis of inspection and testing carried out in 

the manner prescribed by the Council , 


1 . Short title and commencement.- - ( 1 ) These rules may 
be called the Export of Basmati Rice ( Quality Control and 
Inspection ) Rules, 1990 ; 


4. Procedure of Inspection. -- (1 ) An cxporter intending to 
export a consignment of Basmati Rice shall give an 
intimation in writing to the Agency furnishing therein details 
of the contractual specifications alongwith a copy of the ex 
port contract or order to enable the Agency to carry out 
inspection in accordance with rule 3 , 


( 2 ) They shall come into force on the date of their publi 
cation in the Official Gazette . 

2 . Definitions. - - In these rules, unless the context otherwise 
requires : 
(a ) Act means the Export ( Quality Control and Ins 

pection ) Act, 1963 ( 22 of 1963 ) ; 
( b ) Agency means any of the Export Inspection Agen 

cies established by the Central Government at 
Bombay , Calcutta , Cochin , Delhi and Madras under 
Section 7 of the Act for inspection or the Agricul 
tural Marking Adviser to the Government of India 
or any other officer authorised on his behalf for 

inspection ; 
(c ) Council means tho Export Inspection Council estab 

lished under Section 3 of the Act; 
( 1 ) " Basmati Rice (Oryza Sativa ) ” shall mean Basmati 

Raw Milled Ricc , Parboiled Rice and Dehuisked and 
Unpolished ( Brown ) Basmati Rice produced in 
India . 


( 2 ) For export of Basmati Rice processed by excrcising 
adequate inprocess quality control as prescribed by the Coun 
cil and the processing unit adjudged as having adequato in 
process quality control drills by a panel of experts constituted 
by the Council for this purpose , the exporter shall also fur 
nish alongwith the intjmation mentioned in sub - rule ( 1 ) a 
declaration that a consignment of Basmati Rice intended for 
export has been processed by tho exercising adequate quality 
control as prescribed by the Council and the consignment con 
forms to the standard specification recognisod for tho plit 
pose . 


( 3 ) Every intimation under sub -rule ( 1 ) shall bc given not 
less than 2 days before the inspection to be carried out at 
the premises situated at the same station where the office of 
the Agency is located and not less than 10 duya before the 
inspection to be carried out at the premises which are not 
situated at the same station where the oflice of the Agency 
is located . 


3 . Basis of Inspection . — The inspection of Basmati Rico 
shall be carried out with a view to seeing that the same con

भारत का राजपत्र : फरवरी 17, 1990/ माष 18, 1911 


477 


( 4 ) On receipt of the intimation under sub -rule ( 1 ) and the 
declaration if any , under sub -rule ( 2 ) , the Agency - - 
(a ) On satisfying itself that the processor has cxerciscu 

adequate quality control as prescribed by the Coun 
cil in this regard to cosure thut the product con 
form to the standard specification recognised for the 
purpose , shall within 2 days issue a certiticate de 
claring the consignment of Basmali Rice as export 

worthy . 
(b ) In case where the processor is not the exporter, the 

consignment shall bo physically verified and such veri 
ficution and / or inspection , if any necessary , shall be 
curricd out by the Agency to ensure that the above 
conditions aro complied with . 


8. Appeal : 
( 2 ) Any exporter aggrieved by the refusal of the Agency 

to issue the certificate of inspection within 10 days 
of such refusal prefer an appeal which shall be 
referred by the Agency to a Panel of Exports con 

stituted for the purpose ; 
( b ) The quorum of the Panel shall be three ; 
(c ) The appeal shall be disposed of within fifteen days 

from its receipt ; 


( d ) The decision of the Panel in such ppead shall be 
final, 

[ F. No. 6( 13) / 87 - ET & EP] 


( 5 ) ( a ) The Agency shall, however , carry out the spot 
check of some of the consignments meant for export and also 
visit thc processing units at regular intervals to verify the 
adequacy of inprocess quality control drill adopted by the 
unit. If the processing unit is found not maintaining the re 
quired quality control measures at any stage of the processing 
or does not comply with the recommendations of the Coun 
cil / Agency , the unit shall be declared as not having adequate 
in process quality control drills . In such cases , the unit , if so 
desires , shall apply afrosh for adjudgement of tho mainte 
nance of adequacy of inprocess quality control drills,

S. O . 1025 and 1026 dated 19th April , 1980 . 
S. 0 . 824 ( E) dated 30th September, 1980 . 
S. O . 3916 dated 15th October, 1983 . 


(b ) In case where the exporter has not declared under sub 
rulc (2 ) that adequate quality control as laid down by the 
Council had been cxercised , the Agency on salisfying itself 
that the consignment of Basmati Rice conforms to the stand 
ard specification recognised for the purpose on the basis of 
inspection and testing carried out as laid down by the Coun 
cil, shall within 2 or 10 days as the case may be, issue a certi 
licate declaring the consignment of Basmati Rico as export 
worthy ; 


Provided that where the Agency is not satisfied , it shall 
refuse to issue a certificate to the exporter declaring the con 
signment of Basmati Rice as exportworthy and shall com 
municate such refusal within 2 or 10 days, as the case may 
be , to the exporter alongwith the reasons thereof , 


( 6 ) Subsequent to certification, the Agency shall have the 
right to reassoss the quality of the consignmeat at any place 
or storage , in transitor at the ports before its actual ship 
ment ; 


( 7 ) In the event of the consignment being found not con 
forming to thc standard specifications at any of these stuges, 
thic certificate of inspection originally issued shall be with 
drawn .

5 . Packing and Murking . --- An exporter intending to pack 
Basmati Rice for export after preparing the consignment as 
per the rule shall pack in standard new gunay bags / canvas 
bags with inner polythono / cardboard cartons multilayer poly 
laminates or as per specific requirements of the buyer . 


The following information shall be stencilled / printed on 
bags / packages : 

1. Name and address of the exporter ; 
2. Variety ; 
3 . Grade ; 
4. Lot Number : 
5. Gross weight and net weight ; 
6 . Product of India ; 
7. Shipment Mark . 


6 . Place of Inspection. -Inspection for the plurpose of these 
rules shall be carried out at the premises of the exporter 
where the goods are offered for inspection , provided that 
accquate facilities exist llicrein for inspection . 


7 . Inspection Fee. - A lee at ilic rates prescribed for thic 
purpose of inspection shall be paid to the Agency as inspec 
tion fee for consignmentwise inspection and inprocess quality 
control respectively .

का . प्रा . 41 4. ---- केन्द्रीय सरकार का गा प्रतीत होता है कि इसमे उपाबद्ध अनुसूची में वर्णित भूमि से कोयला 
अभिप्राप्त करने की संभावना है । 

अतः, अब केन्द्रीय सरकार कोयला धारक क्षेत्र ( अर्जन और विकास ) अधिनियम , 1957 ( 1957 का 20 ) की धारा 
4 की उपधारा ( 1 ) द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए उस भूमि में कोयला का पूर्वेक्षण करने के अपने प्राशय की गूचना 
देती है । 

इस अधिसूचना के अधीन आने वाले क्षेत्र के रेखांक सं . राजस्व / 16/ 89 तारीख 22 जून , 1989 का निरीक्षण नार्दर्न कोल 
फील्ड्स लि . ( राजस्व अनुभाग ) सिंगरौली के कार्यालय में या कलक्टर सिढी ( मध्य प्रदेश ) के कार्यालय में कोयला नियंत्रक - 1 
काउंसिल हाउस स्ट्रीट , कलकत्ता के कार्यालय में किया जा सकता है । 

इस अधिसूचना के अधीन आने वाली भूमि से हितबद्ध सभी व्यक्ति उस अधिनियम की धारा 13 की उपधारा ( 7 ) में 
निर्दिष्ट सभी नक्शों, चार्टी और अन्य दस्तावेजों की अधिसूचना के राजपत्र में प्रकाशित होने की तारीख से नब्बे दिन के भीतर

का , प्रा . 415 . -- केन्द्रीय सरकार ने कोयला धारक क्षेत्र ( मर्जन और विकास ) अधिनियम, 1957 ( 1957 का 30 ) 
की धारा 7 की उपधारा ( 1 ) के अधीन भारत के राजपत्र असाधारण , भाग 2, खंड 3, उपखंड ( 2 ) तारीख 25 फरवरी , 1987 
में प्रकाशित , भारत के ऊर्जा मंत्रालय ( कोयला विभाग ) की अधिसूचना सं . 120 ( अ ) तारीख 25 फरवरी, 1987 धारा उस 
अधिसूचना से संलग्न अनसूची में विनिर्दिष्ट परीक्षेत्र में भूमि और अधिकारों का अर्जन करने के लिए अपने प्राशय की सूचना दी 
थी ; और सक्षम प्राधिकारी ने उक्त अधिनियम की धारा 8 के अनुसरण में केन्द्रीय सरकार को अपनी रिपोर्ट दे दी है । 

और केन्द्रीय सरकार का , पूर्वोक्त रिपोर्ट पर विचार करने के पश्चात और महाराष्ट्र सरकार से परामर्श करने के पश्चात् 
यह समाधान हो गया है कि इससे संलग्न अनुसूची में वर्णित 1132 . 30 हैक्टर ( लगभग ) या 2796. 78 एकड़ ( लगभग ) माप 
वाली भूमि का अर्जन किया जाना चाहिए । 

प्रतः केन्द्रीय सरकार, उक्त अधिनियम की धारा 9 की उपधारा ( 1 ) द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए , यह घोषणा 
करती है कि उक्त अनुसूची में वर्णित 1132 . 30 हैक्टर ( लगभग ) 2796 . 78 एकड़ ( लगभग ) माप वाली भूमि अर्जित की 
जाती है ।

Nw Delhi , 23rd January, 1990 
s. o . 415 : - . Whereas by the notification of the Government of India in the Ministry of Energy ( Department of Coal ) No . S. O . 120KE ) 
dated the 25th February , 1987 under sub -section (i ) of Section 7 of the Coal Bearing Areas (Acquisition and Development) Act, 1957 
( 20 of 1957 ), and published in the Gazette of India, Extra -ordinary, Part- II , Scction 3, Sub-section ( ii) dated the 25th February, 1987 the 
Central Government gave notice of its intention to acquirc land and rights in the locality specificd in the Schedule appended to that 
notification, 

And whereas the competent authority in pursuance of section 8 of the said Act has made his report to the CentralGovernment; 

And whercas the Central Government after considering the report aforesaid and after consulting the Government of Maharashtra 
is satisficd that the lands measuring 1132. 30 hectares ( approximately ) or 2796. 78 acres ( approximately ) described in the schedule 
appended hereto should be acquired . 

Now , therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred by sub -section (i) of Section 9 of the said Act, the Central Government hereby 
declares that the lands mcasuring 1132 . 30 hectaro ( approximately ) or 2776 .78 acres (approximately ) described in the said Schedule are 
hereby acquired , 

The plan No . C - 1 (E ) III/JJR 424 -0988 dated 21st September, 1988 of the area covered by this notification may be inspected in the 
office of the Collector , Yavatimal ( Maharashtra ) or in the office of the Coal Controller , 1, Council House Street, Calcutta or in the office 
of the Western Coalficlls Limited (Revenue Department ), Coal Estate , Civil Lines, Nagpur (Maharashtra

का प्रा०417 . - - कमाय सरकार परमाण ऊर्जा ( पामों का संचालन , 
बनिज समा निर्धारित तत्वों ग्रा हस्तन ) नियम 1981 मी धारा 2 के 
खण्ड ( ) द्वारा प्रदत्त शक्तियों का प्रयोग करते हुए भारत सरकार के 
परमाणु ऊर्जा विभाग में परमाणु खनिज प्रभाग के निदेशना को उपत्र 
नियमों के प्रयोजन के लि ! " लाइम प्राधिकारी नियुक्त मारती है ।

APPEARANCES ; 

Shri M . T . Parikh , Advocaie for the Bank . 
Shri T , R . Mishia , Advocate for the workmen , 

AWARD 
This industrial dispute between State Bank of Saurashtra; 
Surendranagar and the workmen employed under it has 
been referred to me for adjudication under Section 10 ( 1 ) ( d ) 
of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 , by the Government 
of India , Ministry of Labour s Order No . L - 12012 348181 
DJI ( A ) dated 21st August, 1982 . 

2 . The dispute relates to a single demand of the workmen 
which is as under : 
* Whether the action of the management of State Bank 

of Saurashtra in terminating the service of Shri 
Vijubhai Rayabhai Chekhalia , Godown Chowkidar 
Jawahar Chowk Branch . Surendranagar with effect 
from 1- 8 -79 is justified ? If not. to what relief is 

the workman concerned entitled ? " 
3 . The workman concerned has filed statement of claim 
at Ex. 2 contending inter alia that he was appointed its 
sub -staff in the services of the State Bank of Saurashtra 
( hercinafter referred to as "the Bank ) in the month of 
March , 1979 and he resumed his duties on 5th March , 
1979 . He then continued to work as sub- staff and his ser 
vices were then arbitrarily and abruptly terminating on 
31st July , 1979 afternoon that during the tenure of his 
service his conduct was very good . He was not given 
any show cause notice or there was no 
reason for coniplaint against him ; that he was requesting 
the management for confirmation in the service of the 
Bank as he had completed three month s service in the 
Bank as per the Awards and Settlements in vogue; that he 
was appointed on permanent clear vacancy ; that the Bank 
got annoyed at his request and then terminated his services 
as stated above; that the action taken against him is puni 
live and he has been victimised with ulterior motive . He 
has , therefore , requested that he may be reinstated on his 
original post of sub -staff with full back wages and continuity 
of service and also the fringe benefits which were available 
to him . 


5 . The Bank had prodliced at Ex . 20 the certificais 
showing the number of days worked by the woikman con 
Curned. The said certificate shens that the workman Cou 
cerned had worked as a teniporary gojown chowkidar for 
149 days only . Then by Ex. 2 colleciively various appoint 
ment letters have been also produced which clearly shot! 
that the workman concerned was working the temporary 
godown chowkidar for different fixed periods. 1 Bark 
has also produced the certificate produced by the woi. * 
mian concerned about his heing a candidate from the 
Vaghari community which is socially and economically back , 
ward community. Then at Ex . 7 circular No. C141 dated 
29 - 4 - 1976 giving the guidelines about making appointntents 
and qualifications laid down has also been produje lirin 
No. 2 of Ex. 7 is the copy of the application date: 5 - 3 - 1979 
made hy the workman concerned for getting a jon in the 
Bank . Then iteın No. 3 of Ex. 7 is the appointment letter 
dated 5 - 3 1979 given to the workman concerned . This letter 
clearly show s that the workman concerned was 120insed 
as temporary godown chowkidar . The said letter when shows 
that he was given a purel.y temporary job for a period of 27 
day s only and that too during the absence on leave of one 
godown chowkidai on Laxmi Potteries. The said letler also 
mentions very clearly that he will be discontinued on the 
expiry of the aforesaid period or return to duiy of De 
employee on lcave . The other important condition in the 
said letter was that the temporary appointment, unless 
terminated orally will automatically cease at the expiry of 
the period of his employment and it was also made clear 
that the temporary appointment given to him will not con 
firm on him any right whatsoever to claim absorption in the 
Bank s permanent establishment in any capacity , 


4 . On behalf of the Bank the written statement has been 
filed at Ex. 5 wherein the Bank has contended that the work 
man concerned having been appointed as a temporary 
workman and as the temporary work was over, his services 
had come to an end legally and poperly . It was also stated 
that the workman concerned had not come with clean hands . 
He had suppressed certain materials facts regarding his 
qualifications while seeking temporary appointment. His 
other allegation this his services were terminated because 
he had requested for his confirmation was denied ; that it 
was also denied that he was entitled to be made permanent 
under the provisions of the Awards and Settlements as 
alleged by him . The Bank also denied that the services of 
the workman concerned were illegally and improperly ter 
minated . According to the Bank , the true facts are that 
the workman concerned was employed purely in ihe tem 
porary canacity as godown chowkidar in the godown of 
one of the borrowers of the Bank for the period from 
5 - 3 - 1979 to 31-7 - 1979 . He used to be given appointment 
letters from time to time for his temporary appointment 
and on exnirv of his contractual period he was removed 
from his temporary assignment. He had also not comnleted 
240 days service in the Bank in a calendar year . It was 
also stated that the workman concerned while apnlving for 
the temporarv rost made an anplication dated 5 - 3 - 1979 
and mentioned his educational qualifications as Standard VII. 
However, the Bank subceauently learnt that the workman 
concerned had passed F . Y . B . Com . examination in the year 
1978 ; that as far the rules of the Bank are concerned . 
only non-matriculate can be emplored in the said temporary 


6 . As far as oral evidence is concerned , the workmiri 
has examined himself at Ex. 12 . It apepars from his deroy 
tion that he was appointed as a godown chowkida on 
5 - 3 - 1979 . According to him he was given appointing on 
permanent basis and that he had served for five minths . 
He then stated that at the time of joining the Bank 1 : had 
mentioned about his qualifications. He further states thai 
he has already informed the Bank that he had pas « e : F . Y . B . 
Com ; that from him the work of watchman and also as

Point No. 1 and 2 


These two points have been taken up together for discuis 
sion as they are interconnected . 


Admittedly Sanjib Coke Oven of Sanjib Coke Manufactur 
ing company was nationalised by virtue of Coking CoalMines 
(Nationalisation ) Act , 1972 and the title , interest right and 
the management vested in the Central Government after the 
nationalisation and in duc course it was transferred to M / s . 
BCCL by the Central Government. It is also admitted that 
the owners of Sanjib Coke Manufacturing company filed a 
Writ Petition in the Hon ble High Court of Calcilita challenge 
ing the nationalisation of the Coke oven plant and Sanjib 
Coke Manufacturing company and that the Hon ble High 
Court of Calcutta appointed Shri R . N . Sharma the then 
Managing Director of M / s . BCCL and Shri P . D . Ajmera 
partner of Sanjib Coke Manufacturing company as Joint 
Receivers to operate the Coke oven plants of Sanjib Coke 
manufacturing company till the disposal of the Writ petition 
vide order dated 30 -3 - 72 . Against the said order an appeal 
was filed before the Hon ble High Court at Calcutta and 
the appeilate Court confirmed the order of appointment of 
Joint Receivers and further ordered that Shri Om Prakash 
Gupta the then Administrative Officer of Sanjib Coke Manu 
facturing company was to work as the Manager under the 
Joint Receivers. It is further admitted that the Writ petition 
pending before the High Court of Calcutia was transferred 
to thie Hon ble Supreme Court and the Hon ble Supreme Court 
was placed to decide and dispose off the Writ petition holding 
that the Coking Coal Mines (Nationalisation ) Act, 1972 was 
7 valid piece of legislation vide its order dated 10 - 12 - 82 . Si 
far the case of both the parties are admitted. According 
to the workmen after the said judgement of the Hon ble 
Supreme Court dated 10 - 12 -82 M / s. BCCL took over physical 
possession of the coke oven plant but acc irding to the M / s . 
BCCL the nossession of the Sanjib Coke Oven -plant was not 
given by Shri P . D . Ajmera who in fact was in possession 
of the coke oven plant . According to M / S BCCL , the 
management of Sanjib Coke Manufacturing company was 
evading to hand over the full charge of the plants and machi 
neries books of accounts etc . and as such M / s. BCCL filed 
a case before the Supreme Court and thereafter the Hon ble 
Supreme Court was pleased to direct Shri P . D . Ajmera 
one of the Joint Receiver to hand over the possession of 
plants and machineries , books of accounts etc . vide its 
Order dated 29 - 7 -83 and thereafter Shri Ajmera handed over 
charge of the coke over plant to M / s . BCCL 01 20 - 9 -83 . 
According to the workmen M / s. BCCL came in physical 
possession of Coke Oven plant immediately after judgement 
of the Hon ble Supreme Court dated 10 - 12 - 82 whereas accord 
ing to M / s. BCCL it came in possession of the coke oven 
plant on 20 9 -83 after the order of the Hon ble Supreme Court 
dated 29 - 7 -83 by which Shri Ajmera was directed to hand 
over charge of Coke Oven plant along with the books of 
accounts etc . to BCCL . 


Now the question is whether M / s. BCOT come in 
possession of Sanjib Coke oven plant soon after the decision 
Of Hon ble Supreme Court dated 10 - 12 - 82 . Ext. NL- i is the 
application for direction to the Joint Receiver to hand over 
the possession and management of the business and 
property of the coke oven plants which was numhered as 
Civil Mis . Petit on No . 11026 jf 1982 in the Hon ble 
Supreme Court of India wherein it iz prayed that 
Shri P . D . Ajmera be directed to hand over at Dhanbad 
the possession and management of the business and pro 
perty of the Coke Oven plant to M / s . BCCL along with 
all assets and books of accounts etc . Ext. M - 2 is the 
Order passed in Civil Misc . petition No. 11026 of 1983 
by the Hon ble Supreme Court dated 29- 7 - 83. The order 
shows that Shri P . D . Ajmera was directed to handover pos 
session of all the requisite possession of which he took 
over when he was appointed as Joint Receiver and then 
hand over possession to the Chief Judicial Manager of M / S . 
BCCL . It is clear therefore that as Shri P . D . Ajmera one 
of the Receivers of the Coke oven plants had not handed 
over possession and management of Sanjib Coke oven , 
he was directed by the Hon ble Supreme Court after 
making full deliberation to deliver possession and the manage 
ment of the coke oven plants to M /s. BCCL through its 
Finance Officer. MW - 1 Shri R . G . Singh who was , Dy . 
Chief Personnel Manager , Incharge, Man power, at the head 
quarters of BCCL stated that BCCL was not allowed to play 
the role of Joint Receiver by the erstwhile management during 
the Joint Receivership . It will appear that Shri P . D . Ajmera 
who was one of the partners of Sanjib Coke oven 
was appointed as one of the Receivers and Shri Om P akash 
Gupta who was the Administrative Officer of Sanjib Manu - 
facturing Co . was appointed Manager by the Hon ble Calcutta 
High Court during the pendency of the Writ petition and 
it can well be imagined that the persons who were already 
in possession and management of the Coke oven plants were 
given the power to manage the operation of the Coke plant 
ind as such it appears quite probably that Shri Om Prakash 
Gupta and Shri P . D . Ajmera were not allowing the officers 
of BCCL to work as Joint Receiver and the crder of the 
Hon ble Supreme Court in Ext . M - 2 is clear indication 
that Shri P . D . Ajmera and Shri Om Prakash Gupta were 
not allowing BCCL to take possession and management of 
the Coke oven plants after the decision of the Supreme 
Court dated 10 - 12 - 82 . There is no rositive evidence oil the 
part of the workmen that M / s. BCCL had taken physical 
possession and management of the Coke uven plants after 
10 - 12 - 82 or prior to the order dated 29 - 7 - 83 passed by the 
Hon ble Supreme Court. In view of the evidence discussed 
above I hold that M / s. BCCL. had nor come in possession 
of the management of Sanjib Coke Oven prior to 29 - 7 - 83 . 


In para - 11 of the W . S . of the workmen it is stilted that 
M / s. BCCL took charge of the Coke oven plant just after 
the decision of the Supreme Court on 10 - 12- 82 holding the 
validity of the natiolisation Act , but instead of continuing 
the operation and production of hard coke as was being done 
during the regime of Joint Receivers M / s . BCCL stopped the 
production and engagement of the worhers of the sild coke 
oven plants without any notice . It will thus appear as 
stated by the workmen that Sanjib Coke Oven plant stopped 
production in the month of December , 1982 . WW - 1 Ram 
Sakal Ram and WW - 2 Aftab Ahmed who are two of the 
concerned persons in the reference have stated that they had 
worked till 15 - 12 - 82 and that on 15 - 12- 82 the work of the 
coke oven was stopped by the officers of BCCL. Thus it is 
the lefinite case of the workmen that Sanjib Cohe oven 
was closed in December, 1982. It is the adoriited case of 
the parties that Sanjib Coke oven was unrcr te Receivership 
during the pendency of ihe Writ petition in which the Coking 
Coal Mines (National sacion ) Act was challenged and that the 
said Wsit petition was decided by the Hon ble Supreme 
Court on 10 - 12 - 82 . It is evident therefore that prior 10 
10 - 12 82 idmittedly M / s. BCCL was not in possession of 
Sanjib Coke oven plant and that the said plant was under the 
Joint Receivership appointed by Calcutta High Court . 


As discussed above it will appear from the case of the 
parties that Sanjib Coke oven was closed in December , 
1982 . This fact is further supported by Ext. M - 35 
dated 19 - 2 - 83 is letter from Shri R . N . Sharma, 
Joint Secretary of RCMS to the Chairman -cum -Vanaging 
Director M /s . BCCL Koyala Bhawan , Dhanbad relating to 
the employment of th : workmen of Sanjib Cole Manufactur 
ing Company. It is stated in this letter that the private 
management has clo ed the Coke oven and have filed away 
and that the management of M / s . BCCL has also not taken 
over possession of the sa d coke oven plant and as such about 
90 workmen who were engaged in the coke oven in different 
jobs are idle since 16 12 - 82. Thus it is clear from that this 
letter of Shri R . N . Sharma, Toint Secretary of RCMS who 
appears to be in know of the situation that Sanjib Coke oven 
had been closed by the erstwhile owner of Sanjib Coke oven 
and that M / s. BCCL had not laken its possession . Tu clearly 
supports the facts that the coke oven plants was closed by 
the erstwhile owner of Sanjib Coke oven prior to 16 - 12 - 82. 
I hold therefore that Sanjib Coke oven plant was closed by 
the private erstwhile management prior to 16 - 12 - 82 . 


I have held above that Sanjib Coke oven plant was closed 
prior to 16 - 12 - 82 by the owner of Sanjih Coke Oven and 
that the management of M ! s. BCCI took its possession and 
management in a closed condition after 29 - 7 - 83 . 


ſ further hold that the Coke Oven plant of Sanjeev Coke 
Manufacturing Company was in a closed condition at the 
time BCCI took its possession Thus fo nt Nos 1 and 2 
are accordingly decided .

wag anomolous . Letter A are linked togethor in the 
cntries against Sl. No. 37 of the writing marked & 
114 which is anomolous. The above anachronistic fca 
ture shows the spuriotis nature of the 2 attendance Rerig 
ters Fxt. M - 24 and M -241. He report also shows that 
the portion stamped and marked by him as Q 1 to 46 
were all written by one and the same person. The sald

The strange thing about this document is that they purport

after June, 1982 it is evident that the entries shown in 
these documents regarding payment of wages during the

written after June, 1982 and as such these documents are 
entirely spurious and fabricated for showing that the waves 
had been paid and the concerned persons had given their

of payment of wages . 


MW - 2 , Dipankar Gupta is working as Dy. Chief Engi 
neer ( Coke ) BCCL . He is specialised in Hard çoke mak 
ing and is the Head of the Coul Projects department of 
BCCL . He hud visited Sanjeov Coke Oven in 1983 and 
found it in closed condition . He had found 42 Ovens and 
21 chimneys in Sanjcev Coke Oven at that time, According 
to him the full production capacity of the said oven and 
Chimney can be 40 tonnes per day and for that 60 to 70 
nersons were required for its workiog Sanjeev Hard Coke 
is a bohive coke which has no by -product. He has stated 
that it is not necessary for RCCI. to open the working of 
Sanjeev Coke Oven considering the short demand of hard 
cohe and the loss to be sustained. According to the case 
of the workmen also the production of coke was about 
50 tonnes per day . W - 1 has stated that 4 - 1 / 2 tonnes of 
Coul used to be put in each Oven of Sanjcev Coke Oven 
pants. He has stated that llic Coke used to be taken out 
from the Coke Oven on the 4th day after 72 hours. He 
hun states thut there were 42 Oven in Sanjeev Haril Cake 
Bhatta and that some of the Oven are also in repairs 
sometimes. This it appears from his evidence also that 
the number of 184 concerned persons was not required to 
work the sait coke oven . 


Considering the entire evidenc discussed above it will 
appear that the only reliable cvidence in respect of the 
contractor s worker s cmployed in Sanjeev Coke Osca pints 
: npear in Ext, M - 38 containing the list of the workers of 
Shri Ekhal Ahment contractor engaged in the cok oven 
plant It hears the signālure of the contractor Elhal

Ram Sukal Rom one of the concerncat workman and Shri

cannot say that was not an admitted list of the contractors 
workers. Accordingly I hold that the said concerned rer 
sons nanırd in Ext. M - 38 were the contractors workers 
working in Sanjeev Coke Oven ile on 15- 12 - 1982 


In view of the above fact that the Attendance Registers 
Ext. M - 24 series and the wage Register Ext. M - 23 scries 
are fabricated and purious documents the same cannot 
be accepted in support of the case of the concerned per 
sons and it has to be held as submitted by the management 


I hold therefore that the concernel 71 per Ons named 
in Ext, M - 38 were the contractors workers and that they 
were not the departmental workers of Sunleev Coke Manu 
facturing. Thus roint No. 4 are accordingly decided .

The case of the management is that the concerned work

right . It is admitted that the two concerned workmen were

and Executive Engineer of the Project on tlic recommcncla

nees may be regularised in Excavation Group - E as Auto Elec 
trician helper on successful completion of one year service AS 
helper . It was also decided that their case of regular place 
inent in the appropriate grade will be made after adjudging 
suitability through practical trade test. Immediately after

neither eligible vor qualified for regularisation in excavation 
Executive Group - D . According to the management there is 
no morit in the demand of the workmen . It was not a condi

In the matter of an industrial dispute under Section 10 ( 1)(d ) 

of the I. D . Act, 1947

Kankunce of Sijua rea No . V of M / s. BCC Ltd . 
and their workmen . 


The only point for decision in this case is whether the 
concerned workinan should be placed us Auto Electrician 
Vixcavation in Excavation Group D with effect from 11- 2 - 82 . 


APPEARANCES : 


On hehalf of the workmen : Shri K . D . Prasad, Secre 

tary , C . M . E . W . A . Union . 
On behalf of the employers : Shri G , Prasad , Advocate . 
STATE : Bihar, 

INDUSTRY : Coal. 


The management and the workmen cach exanined one 
witness in support of their respective case . The documents 
of the management are marked Ext. M - 1 to M - 7 and the 
documents of the workmen are marked Ext, W - 1 to W - 10 . 


Duted , Dhanbud , the 12th January , 1990 


AWARD 


The Government of India , Ministry of Labour in cxcrcise 
of the powers conferred on them under Section 10 ( 1 ) (d ) of 
thc 1, D . Act, 1947 has referred the following dispute to 
this Tribunal for adjudication vide their Order No. J .-24012 / 
1 /87 - D .I ( B ) dated , the 20th July , 1987 . 


The dispute in the case is nowy melted down to the point 
whether the concerned workman should be given the differ 
ence of wages of Group & and Group D from 14 - 3 - 82 vinco 
the management vido its office order dated 26 - 5 - 89 ordered 
that thc concerned workman Shri Irfan Ansari will get senio 
rily in Excavation Group D with cffect from 14 - 3 -82 . This 
office order Ext. W - 9 was passed during the hearing of this 
reference and is such now there is no dispute about the 
point whether the concerned workman should get Excavation 
Group D with effect from 14 - 3 - 82 when he had completed 
one year of his training period . However the ofhce Order 
Ext. W -9 shows that the effective financial benefit will be on 
and from 11- 11- 86 AS approved by the competent authority . 
Thus vide this office order the management ordered not to 
pay the wages of Group D and the difference of wages of 
Group E and Group D from 14 - 3 -82 to 10 - 11 - 86 . 


SCHEDULE 


Whether the action of the Management of P . R .S . P . 

Kankanee of Sijua Area No . V of M /s . - Bharat 
Coking Coal Ltd ., Dhanbad in not placing Sri Mohd. 
Irfan Ansari, Auto Electrician Excavation in Exca 
vation Group D with effect from 11 - 2 - 1982 is justi 
fied ? If not, to what relief the concerned workman 
is entitled ? " 


The case of the workman is that Shri Mh , Irfan Ansari 
was appointed as an Auto Electrician ( Trainee ) vide appoint 
mcnt Jetter dated 14 - 3 -81 in Group E . He was to be under 
training for one year and thereafter he was to be given 
his scale of pay of Excavation Group as Auto Electrician . 
He was posted to Nichitpur 0 . C . P . vide order dated 14 -4 -81, 
After he completed the period of training he was regularised 
as Auto Electrician in Group F with effect from 11 - 4 -82, 
The regularisation of the concerned workmen in Excavation 
Group F was not proper and he should have been regularised 
in Group D excavation as an Auto Electrician with effect 
from 11 -4 - 82 . Excavation Group E is given to Auto Elec 
trician Helper . The concerned workman demanded his regu 
larisation in Excavation Group D and the wages of Group D 
in April, 1982 . He again demandait by letter dated . 1 - 11- 85 
for regularising him in Group D and payment of the wages 
of Group D . Thereafter the union of the concerned work 
man also made a demand for giving Excavation Group D 
and its scale of pay to the concerned workman and also his 
promotion to Excavation Group C . When the management 
did not pay any heed the union raised an industrial disputo 
before the ALCIC ) , Dhanbad and on failurç of the concilia 
tion thc present reference has been made for adjudication . 
It is prayed by the workman that the concerned workman 
should be given Excavation Group D with effect from 11 - 4 - 82 
along with the difference of wages of Group E & D and 
that he should be promoted to Excavation Group ç . 


As the concerned workman has hecn given Excavation 
Group D with effect from 14 - 3 - 82 all other controversies 
whether the concerned workman was entitled to Group D 
after one year of his period of training or not no longer 
exists. It will also appear that cicavation Group Ę is given 
to Auto Electrician Helper and Group D is given to an Auto 
Electrician . MW I Shri R . B . P . Sahi is a Sr. Personnel Omicer . 
He has stated in his cross - examination that the concerned 
workman was appointed as Auto Electrician Excavation 
traincc and that auto Electricians are in Excavation Group 
D . He has further stated that the concerned workman is 
working regularly and continuous y us Auto Electrician . It is 
clear therefore that the concerned workman was regularly 
working as Auto Electrician and continuously uficr the period 
of his training and this fact also will show that the con 
cerned workmen was to be placed in Excavation Group D 
und he should have been paid the scale of pay of Excava 
tion Group D . The management has given no reason in 
Ext. W - 9 As tu why the effective date of financial benefit 
was given to the concerned workman from 11 - 11- 86 . The 
cvidence of the management itself shows that the concerned 
workman was regularly and continuously working as Auto 
Electrician and as such there is no reason as to why he 
should not be given the waves of Excavation Group D with 
effect from 14 - 3 -82. Ext. W 10 is 211 uffice order dated 
22 / 23 - 3 - 88 which shows that Auto Electriciany who had jojn 
ed as trainees in 1982 and had completed one year of their 
training in 1983 were given Excavation Group D in 1983 
as soon as they had completed one year of the training . 
Fxt , W - 10 shows that the management had given Excavation 
Group D after completion of one year of the period of 
training and in their case there is no subsequent date of 
effective date for financial benefit. It will thus aprear that 
the Cusc of the concerned workman is heing discriminated 
against him whereas in the case of others us shown in Ext . 


The case of the management is that the workman have 
heen demanding promotion which is the function of the 
management and cannot he claimed as a matter of right. It 


520 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA : FEBRUARY 17 , 1990 /MAGHA 28 , 1911 


[ PART II-- Sec . : (ii) ] 


L 


W 10 others were given Excavation Grade D just after con 
pletion of one year of the poriod of training . In this view 
of the mattei I hold that the conscried workman desorves 
1hc scalc of ray of Excavation Group D with effect from 
14 - 3 - 82 and that he should be paid the difference of wages 
of Excavation Group E and Excavation Group D from 
14 - 3 - 82 . 


AWARD 
The Government of India , Minjatry of Labour in cxor 
cise of the powers conforred on them by clause (d ) of sub 
section ( 1) and sub - section (2A ) of Section 10 of the Indus 
trial Disputos Act, 1947 has referred the following disputo 
to this Tribunal tur adjudication under Order No. 1 - 22012 
18 ) /89- IR ( C - II ) dated 18- 7 - 1989. 


SCHEDULE 


In the result , I hold that the action of the management 
of P . R . S . P . Kankance of Sijua Arca No. V of M / s, BCCL 
in not placing the concerned workman Shri Md. Irfan Ansari 
Auto Electrician Excavation in Excavilion Group D with 
cffect from 14 - 3 - 82 is not justified . As now the concerned 
workman has been placed in Excavation Group D with effect 
from 14 - 3 -82 there is no need to pass any direction to placc 
in Excavation Group D . The management however , is direct 
ed to pay the difference of wages of Excavation Group E 
and Excavation Group D from 14 - 3 - 82 to 10 - 11 - 86 to the 
concerned workman within one month froin the date of 
publication of the Awarch .

3 . The Management of the Bank took the plea in its writ 
ten statement that the workman Shri Sahoo had simply been 
empanelled alongwith four others for engagenient as substi 
tute on rotation against available leave vacancies. The sec 
ond party Shri Sahoo was being engaged as a subsiitute sub 
staff against leave vacancies . It was stated that the total 
period for which the second party Shri Sahoo was engaged 
in the Bank was 84 days between 12 - 5 - 1980 when he was 
empanelled and 9 - 4 - 1982 when his name was caucelled from 
the Panel. It is the plea of he Management that the second 
party was not required to work continuously and acquired 
no right to continuous employment. His engagement was 
intermittent and he was being paid wages on daily rate for 
the days he worked against eave vacancies. The further case 
of the Management of the Bank is that the work and perfor 
mance of the second party shii Sahoo during his engagement 
as substitute was found unsatisiiectely and therefore , it was 
decided not to engage him in future leave vacancies and 
accordingly his name was removed from the Panel an order 
dated 23- 4 - 1982 . It is disclosed in the managernent s written 
statement that the second party Shri Sahoo filed a suit in the 
Court of the Subordinate Judge , Puri seeking a declaration 
that the action of the Management of the Bank was illegal 
and for decree for Rs. 3 , 360 / - towards back wages and com 
pensation . The suit was contested . In course of hearing of 
the suit prayer was made by Shri Sahoo to transfer the suit 
to the Central Administrative Tribunal, Cuttack Branch under 
Section 29 of the Administrative Tribunal Act , 1985. The 
Subordinate judge passed an order on 22 - 7 -1987 recording 
his finding that he had no jurisdiction to try the suit in view 
of the provisions of the Administrative Tribunal Act , 1985 
and ordered return of the plaint to the Centra ! Administrative 
Tribunal. Thereafter the second party approached the Assis 
tant Labour Commissioner (Central) , Bhubaneswar for inter 
vention and the Asst. Labour Cominissioner (Central), Bhu 
baneswar wrongly accepted and admitted the same to conci 
liation . In the written statement of the Management of the 
Bank it is stated that there was neither any appointment to 
the post so far as the second pality Shri Sahoo was concerned 
and there was no removal irorú any post. The name of Shui 
Sahoo was merely removed from the panel of the waiteis 
for short time engagement. The employer employee rela 
tionship between the Management of the Bank and the sec 
old party was only limited to the spells of his engage:nent 
as a substitute and not beyond that.

AWARD 


The Government of India in the Ministry of Labour, in 
exercise of the powers conferred upon them by clause (d ) 
of sub - section ( 1 ) of section 10 of the Industrial Disputes 
Act, 1947 (17 of 1947 ), and by their Order No. L12012 / 
89787- D . II ( A ) , dated 10th July , 1988 bave referred the 
following dispute for adjudication by this Tribunal : 


" Whether the action of the minagement of Indian Bank , 

Puri Branch in terminating the services of Shri 
Biswanath Sahoo , Sub -stuff is justified ? If not, to 
what relief the workm : n is entitled ? " . 


in an additional written Statement the Management of the 
Bank referred to the Administrative instructions issued by the 
Bank from time to timne regarding engagement of persons 
during leave vacancies of sub -staff and on the basis theieof 
it was contended that the maximum age limit for final engage 
ment of Persons during leave vacancies of sub- staff shall be 
30 years and such empanelled Persons can not be employed 
further against leave vacancies on attaining the age of 30 
years . It was also contended on the basis thereof that the 
second party Shri Sahoo s date of birth is 2 - 8 - 1952 and there 
fore , he was entitled for further engagement against leave 
vacancies only till 1 - 8 - 1982 and not beyond . 


4 . On the pleadings of the parties, the following issues were 
framed : 


2 . It is the can of the second party - workinan that he had 
been empanchieu by the Manager of the Bank for engage 
ment as substitute sub -staff against available leave vacancies 
and he worked as a sub - staff Peon regularly giving attendance 
every day. On 23 - 4 - 1982 on the plea that his services were 
not required in future , he was teinuinated . The second party 
workman contended that the plea that he was surplus was 
false , in as much as, after termination of his employment 
the bank has filled up the vacancies of the post of sub -staff 
Peons by recruiting outsiders. He alleged that termination 
of his services was not a case of termination simplicitor but 
infact, it was retrenchment. He also alleged that the Mana 
gement of the Bank had repeatedly « lleged that his coriduct 
and behaviour were thoroughly insatisfactory , that his ser 
vices were found deliinental and prejudiciul to the interest 
of the Bauk , that his behaviour way unruly and that he was 
chisobedient ind ilslibordinate it on the basic of those alle 
gations his services were terminated without being given any 
opportunity to evplain away the allegations against him 


( 1 ) If the second party was a workman under the 

First Party -Management within the meaning of 
Section 2 (s ) of the Industijal Disputes Act, 1947 ? 


( 2 ) 


If the dispute that has been referred for adjudication 
is not an industrial dispute between the First Party 
Management and the secund Party 


( 3 ) 


If there has put l een any termination of ercarloy 
ment of the second party by the First Party -oned 
Management as alleged

PART 11 - Sec . 3 (ii) 


(4 ) 


If the termination or cmployment of the second 
party Shri Biswanath Sahoo by the First Party - 
Management was an act of retrenchment or an act 
of discharge simpliciter ? 


( 5 ) 


If such termination of employment of the second 
party - workman is legal and / or justified ? 


(6 ) 


To what relief, if any, the second party-- workman 
is entitled ? 


5 . ISSUE NO . 1 : 


First Party Bank to the Zonal Manager of the Bank at 
Calcuita . In this letter it 1173 mentioned that the second 
party Shri Sahoo was one of the inembers of the captioned 
Panel maintained by the Puri Oliice and he was appointed 
temporarily from time to time. His performance of duty and 
behaviour were found to be fai from salislactury and as such , 
it had been decided not to employ him any further . In this 
letter confirmation was sought for from the Zonal Manager 
and by Ext. 6 the Zonal Manager directed that the second 
party need not be engaged in future leave vacancies and his 
name be removed from the panel of temporary sub -staff after 
duly informning him . In the written statement - -Ext. 7 filed 
by the Management of the Bank in the suit filed by the sec 
ond party Shri Sahoo in the Court of the Subordinate Judge , 
Puri seeking a declaration tha : the action of the Manage 
ment of the Bank was bad and claiming back wages and 
compensation , the Malagemunt look the plea that the rela 
tionship between the plaintiff (the second party Shri Sahoo ) 
and the defendant ( the First Party Management , namely , 
the Indian Bank ) was plisely one of master and servant and 
as such , the suit was not competent. It was also stated in 
the said written statement that Shri Saitoo was engaged at 
the relevant point of ime purely on temporary basis termi 
nable at any time without notice . Ext . 8 is a copy of the 
r anel containing the name of Shri Sahoo and four others 
and Shri Sahoo s position was No . 1 in the said Panel. 


It is the plea of the First Party - - Management that the 
second party Shri Sahoo nas simp y been empanelled to be 
engaged as a substitute Peon against leave vacancies as and 
when required and that too in rotation with such other enipa . 
nelled persons and therefore he was not a workman within 
the meaning of the Industrial Disputes Act, Section 2 ( s ) of 
the Industrial Disputes Act defines workman as " any person 
(including an apprentice ) employed in any industry to do any 
manual , unskilled , skil . ed , technical, operational, cierical or 
supervisory work for hire or reward , whether the icrms of 
employment by express, implied and for the purpose of any 
proceeding under this Act in relation to an industrial dispute , 
includes any such person who has been dismissed , discharged 
or retrenched in connection with , or as a consequence of that 
dispute , or whose dismissal, discharge or retrenchment has 
led that dispute ,.. .. .. ... .. . " . To appreciate the question as to 
whether the second party Shri Sahoo was a workman under 
the Management of the First Party -- Management, before 
going through the oral evidence , we may look into the ap 
pointment letter Ext. 1 dated 9th May , 1980 . This letter 
was issued by the Manager of the Bank , Puri branch to the 
second party - workman . The subject of the letter has been 
mentioned as " Appointment of Temporary Sub -staff in our 
office ." It is stated in this letter that the Bank intended to 
appoint a few sub -staff purely on temporary basis to work 
in place a permanent sub - staff who goes on leave or on duty 
to other places. It is further mentioned in the said letter 
that the name of the second party had been sponsored by the 
local employment exchange for interview which was schedule 
to be held at the Office of the Bank on 14 - 5 - 1980. The 
second party was requested in that letter to appear at the 
interview alongwith his certificate in original. Ext. 2 is the 
termination letter dated 23 - 4 - 1982 which was issued to the 
second party workman by the Manager of the Bank in which 
it was stated that under instructions from the Zonal Oflice , 
Calcutta his name have been removed from the panel of 
temporary sub - staff maintained in the Bank . He was also 
informed that his services will not be required in future. A

6 . M . W . 1 , who was the Branch Manager of the Puri 
Branch of the Indian Bank from December , 1979 to January , 
1982 stated that the local employment Exchange , Puri had 
been requested by him to furnish a list of twenty ive Candi 
dates to be selected to work as sub - staff on temporary basis 
against leave vacangies . The Employment Exchange sent a 
list out of which five candidates were selected to te em 
panelled for such temporary appointment against leave 
vacancies. Sri Sahoo was one of them . Such entpanelled 
persons were engaged whenever leave vacancy arose . One 
person out of the list was appointed against such vacancy 
and after the permanent incumbent returns on expiry of his 
leave , he is disengaged . He stated that the second party 
was disengaged because his behaviour was not found to be 
satisfactory and on 23 -4 - 82 he was disengaged and disem 
panelled from the list. He also stated that out of the 
select list Ext. B in which the second party Sri Sabou had 
been placed at No. 1 position , Sri Purna Chandra Patra 
and Laxmidhar Bhoi, who were juniors to him have bcen 
appointed against regular posts . The second party Sri Saboo 
examined as W . W . 1 stated that he was engaged daily in 
the bank . 


7 . From the evidence on records it appears that the 
Management of the Bank obtained the names of the candi 
dates from the local Employment Exchange , held an inter 
view and selected five out of the candidates sponsored , who 
were empanelled for being appointed against leave vacancies 
as and when required . The second party -worknar stood 
first in the interview and in the list of empanelment occupi 
ed the No . 1 position . This being his status , whether it 
could be caid that he was a workman within the neaning 
of the Industrial Disputes Act ? On going through the 
definition of the expression workman as given in Section 
2 ( s ) of the Industrial Disputes Act, I will have no hesita 
tion to hold that he is a workman coming within the 
definition . By being selected in the interview to be em 
panelled he got a right to be employed against leave 
vacancies and infact , he worked in the Bank agailist leave 
vacancies as a sub - staff Peon . The principle of interpreta 
tion of the definition of workman and industrial dispute 
have been elaborately stated in the case of S . K . Verna, 
Appellant V . Mahesh Chandra and another , Respondents , 
reported in A . I.R . 1984 S . C . 1462. It has been stated in 
the aforesaid decision that interpretation of the aforesaid 
definitions found in the Industrial Disputes Act siroult be 
made so as not to whittle down but to advance the object 
of the Act which is to bring aliulil peace and harmony bet 
been lalir and management. Nüllow and resuicted mean 
ing must not be given to ile aforesaid delinitions so as to 
exclude the labour and management from the operation of 
the Act .
